# Supporting women business leaders and women’s employment in Sierra Leone

CFM’s investment concerns the development of a waste to value portfolio in the city of Freetown, Sierra Leone. The assets comprise modular units which process organic waste and generate clean energy from it.

The project is expected to create 10 jobs for women, provide training opportunities for women service managers to put in place a resourced plan for the on-going cleaning and maintenance of the facilities, and which does not default to traditional gender roles.


## Box 1. Supporting women business leaders and women’s employment in Sierra Leone

The waste is collected by a group of young women (youth groups), who were established, trained and equipped by Freetown City Council to operate door-to-door household waste collection in the city, and are jointly responsible for collecting up to 50% of the waste in the city. The youth groups also collect recyclable materials from premises and returning customers including a women’s maternal health centre in Freetown.

The company in which CF is investing is a woman-led business as it is their partner organisation. Female operators are actively sought, and the youth groups are approximately 30% female. With C2’s investment, the company will be able to expand its operations into other communities of community development that includes a focus on women as well as youth groups.

As regards the protection and management of wetlands, coastal and marine ecosystems, projects supporting sustainable practices in ocean-based industries show how only taking account such restrictions – an ever-shrinking time of mobility, for instance – but actively seeks to tackle discriminatory norms which limit women’s livelihoods opportunities.

Project C2 will also provide training and support to climate-resilient port operations, and support their integration and progression, by providing a combination of initiatives to build their skills and confidence and provide a supportive work environment.


### Understanding the results and impact of gender mainstreaming

Monitoring and reporting will be undertaken in accordance with the principles set out in CFM’s Environmental and Social Management System (ESMS). The ESMS is designed to monitor and evaluate the actions taken to promote gender equality, their results, and impacts. In two ways: Firstly, by monitoring and evaluating key impact indicators at the Fund level. Secondly, by implementing monitoring plans for individual programmes.

### Fund Level Monitoring and Evaluation

The KPIs established for individual programmes will supplement the key impact indicators that have been adopted for the C2 funds, and against which C2 is committed to monitor performance in all


---
**Page 237**
---

projects. These indicators are linked to select UN Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). The impact indicators that are relevant to gender are shown in Table 3 below.

| Table 2 | G2 Impact Indicators |
| --- | --- |
| **UN SDG** | **Gender-Balanced G2 Two-Impact Indicators** |
| SDG 5 - Gender Equality | * Number of full time, equivalent female employees. |
| SDG 6 - Clean Water and Sanitation | * Number of females in management positions. |
| SDG 8 - Decent Work and Economic Growth | * Time saved by women and girls due to improved access to clean drinking water and sanitation. |
|  | * Number of new direct jobs created, gender disaggregated. |
|  | * Catalyst effect (EUE income) total (all and commercial financing). |

Project level monitoring and evaluation

Project companies will implement a monitoring and evaluation plan, which sets out:

* The indicators to be used to monitor how women and girls, as well as any other social priority groups e.g., indigenous women, women from chronically poor households and women with disabilities are being affected—both positively and negatively—by project interventions, and the impact of these on their livelihoods.
* The methodology for gathering and analysing monitoring data, as well as reporting and using this information.

Each monitoring and evaluation plan will be bespoke to individual projects. However, they will contain comparable quantitative and qualitative indicators and data collection methodologies where appropriate. This will allow for comparison across projects at regular intervals to understand progress made during project implementation. The aim of project monitoring is to provide timely information to make project adjustments and address any negative effects.

Relevant components of the monitoring and evaluation plan may be incorporated into contracts for EPC or other contractors, with review of the plan's implementation forming part of the contractor’s normal review process.

There is a need for more evidence on effective approaches to promoting gender equality through investments in infrastructure. The G2 Fund supports such efforts within port infrastructure. In response to this, CII FM will publish some of the results and learning from its G2 investments.


Creating an enabling environment for gender mainstreaming in the CII Fund and the Project Companies

CFCM recognises the need to draw on specialist skills and advice to guide the company’s leadership on how to operationalise its gender commitments across the business and within the investments it manages. To this end, CFCM has established a network of gender specialists and advisors who can be hired on an as-needed basis to provide both fund and project-specific support. This network will benefit from contacts with local organisations with a strong track record in addressing gender issues in infrastructure projects. The network will also have access to information about climate-resilient port infrastructure. It is anticipated that these specialists and local organisations could


---
**Page 238**
---

potentially partner with Project Companies as a delivery agent e.g., in managing the stakeholder consultation with women or in community development activities.

CFM will support Project Companies to put in place essential internal systems and capacities that will facilitate gender equality commitments being embedded into operations, thereby operationalising this O2 Fund Gender Action Plan. Arrangements will include:

- Developing a local (project-specific) gender action plan based on an analysis of gender-related challenges and opportunities within the project area, which will integrate project-specific and organisational intentions for promoting gender equality.
- Agreeing and implementing a project-specific Gender Equality Policy which sets out the commitment to gender equality in all aspects of the project’s operations and how it will be implemented. This policy will set out the Project Company's position on gender mainstreaming and any specific measures required to address Sexual Violence and Harassment (SVH) and the mechanisms put in place to safeguard staff from it.
- Taking proactive measures to recruit a gender-balanced project team, seeking, where possible, to recruit qualified women into roles traditionally held by men.
- Building the understanding and support of company staff to promote gender equality within their organisational context and in the project itself.
- Integrating expectations of gender equal behaviours into staff job specifications and performance agreements. This will include a clear statement from senior management commitment to gender equality and setting Project Company expectations in this regard.
- Providing the project team responsible for health, safety, social and environmental implementation with guidance on how they can support the Project Company in the effective implementation of its gender equality commitments.
- Implementing and managing a grievance mechanism for staff to report complaints, including cases of sexual harassment or violence.
- Monitoring and reporting progress against agreed indicators, tracking the impacts of the project on women and girls.

Project Companies will be expected to require their principal contractors[1] to give due attention to gender issues in their own operations. Requirements to be imposed on contractors are likely to include:

- An operational Gender Equality Policy
- Proactive measures to recruit a gender-balanced team, seeking, where possible, to recruit qualified women into roles traditionally held by men.
- A grievance mechanism for the contractor (and sub-contractor) workforce to report complaints of sexual harassment or violence.

These requirements would form part of the contract. Progress on delivering against these requirements would form part of the normal contractor performance review process.


[1] In many cases, the project is built by one principal contracting company – referred to as the Engineering, Procurement and Construction (EPC) contractor. In other cases a split contract may be awarded to different principal contracting parties. In such cases, each party would be required to follow the same requirements, with the actions coordinated by the Project Company.

Itad is a global organisation. Our strategy, monitoring,
evaluation and learning services work to make international
development more effective. We generate evidence on
important issues – from malnutrition to migration – to support
our partners to make informed decisions and improve lives.
